# FIX-156 Round 2 cross-examination: is the preparation implementable as a Replay-only fix with no Production code changes?

This was a read-only review. I changed no code, configuration, database, git state or preparation file. I also couldn't save a plan file: no file-writing or plan-exit tool is available in this session, so this report is the whole output.

**Short answer:** not freeze-ready. Codex and I agree it should be rejected. Below I correct two overstated Codex claims and three of my own Round-1 claims; the corrections change how serious some findings are, not the verdict.

## FINDINGS

### Where I agree with Codex (checked against the code)

**C1. AGREE: the required specification file still holds an older draft.**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md:3` is `RPR-0.4-DRAFT`, and every approval line (`:315-317`) is PENDING.
- The preparation admits this itself (`FIX-156_IMPLEMENTATION_PREPARATION.md:427`).
- The reviewed RPR-0.9.12 lives in Downloads, outside the repository. Its hash proves nothing about the file the governance rules require.

**C2. AGREE, with a refinement: "Section 3.3.1" is a broken reference.**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** the preparation cites it at `:152`, `:167` and `:168` but never defines it.
- **AGENT INFERENCE:** the section probably exists inside RPR-0.9.12. Either way, the order in which TP, stop-loss, profit-lock, rebase and near-TP exits are checked (what tests T156-02a/b must match) isn't anywhere in the repository. This is a consequence of C1, not a separate defect, but it is blocking.

**C3. AGREE: R156-05 and R156-06 name shared Production classes as the things to change.** This matches my Round-1 point F2.

### Where Codex overstated

**C4. CHALLENGE: Codex says equal-time price ordering "cannot currently be certified."**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `MarketPriceEventService.find` (`:118-128`) already:
  - keeps only accepted prices (`delivery_status IN ('LEGACY','APPLIED')`);
  - orders by `observed_at ASC, id ASC`.
- `RegressionTestWorker.java:198` passes exactly that list to `replay`. The re-sort at `ShadowProductionReplayService.java:93` is by time only, but Java's `Stream.sorted` keeps the original order for equal times. So the id order for prices at the same instant is preserved in practice today.
- **AGENT INFERENCE:** the real gap is about evidence, not behaviour:
  - `PriceEvent` has no `id` (`:173`), so `PRICE_SPAN`, `priceEventId` and `OPEN_AT_REPLAY_END` (R156-08) have no event id to record;
  - the correct order relies on an implicit stable sort, not an explicit tie-breaker.
- R156-06 is still needed, but as a new Replay-owned reader of `market_price_event`, not a change to the shared projection. Severity: material for logging and certification, not proof that Replay orders prices wrongly today.

**C5. NARROW: Codex's claim that analysis uses a "broad decision graph."** It's correct but partly overstated. See my correction R1 below: regime state does nothing in the baseline run.

### Corrections to my own Round 1

**R1. CORRECTION to my F4: regime state does not carry over between signals in a baseline run.**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `RegimeStateService.java:35` returns early unless the Replay scope is active and in EXPERIMENTAL mode. Baseline runs use `PRODUCTION_PARITY` (`ShadowProductionReplayService.java:99`).
- I withdraw "stateful `regimeStateService.apply`" as a parity input. Sentiment on/off (`AnalysisService.java:227`), fundamentals availability, the prior indicator snapshot, ATR and the market context remain valid points.

**R2. RESOLVED unknown: SELL validation doesn't read wallet settings.**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** `validateSell` (`TradeExecutionValidationService.java:165-191`) only does basic signal checks and looks up recent signals through the Replay scope (`:377`) when the scope is active.
- `wallet_settings` is read only on the BUY path (`:66`, `:152`, via `settings()` at `:400`).
- So rule O-1 is a BUY-only problem. That narrows the choice in my F3: only BUY validation and execution-price revalidation need a fixture-backed mechanism.

**R3. DOWNGRADE of my F6 (Replay thread pool).**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** seven named executor beans already exist (`SharedAnalysisExecutorConfig`, `OrderBookPersistence`, `OrderBookCollection`, `LocalCandleReconciliation`, `Fix154Audit`, `CandleAnalysis`, Г?▌). None is named `taskExecutor`, and there is no `AsyncConfigurer`.
- **AGENT INFERENCE:** plain `@Async` methods already find no single default executor. Adding one more named executor doesn't change how they are resolved. Only a bean named `taskExecutor`, an `AsyncConfigurer`, or a `spring.task.execution` property change would.
- Severity drops from MATERIAL to MINOR. Keep one requirement in R156-02: no bean named `taskExecutor`, no `AsyncConfigurer`, use `@Async("<replay-name>")`.
- **UNVERIFIED:** I haven't checked how Spring Boot 4.1 resolves the default async executor.

**R4. DOWNGRADE of my F5 (synthetic signal IDs).**
- **PROJECT EVIDENCE:** across the codebase, `signalRepository.save` appears only in `AnalysisService` (`:123`, `:153`, `:175`). `analyzeForRegression` is read-only and returns an unsaved signal (`:180-190`).
- The write risk is lower than I said. A test that no repository write is reachable is still required, because `TradeSignal` is a JPA entity.

### Blockers still standing under the "no Production code" rule

- **B1. The mechanism isn't chosen.** For BUY validation, D50Г?"D52 and analysis inputs, the preparation must pick between:
  - copying the logic into Replay, which then needs a drift-detection test; or
  - building unmodified Production classes with Replay-owned fixture dependencies.
  
  Neither is in the registry.
- **B2. No file-ownership rule.** No R156 item lists exact files, and there is no acceptance check that the diff touches no Production-owned file.
- **B3. Two rulings are needed from Jean:**
  - Does redeploying the single application, which includes Replay, count as a Production change? This touches the unresolved release gate on protecting open positions during redeploy.
  - Does an additive Flyway migration (R156-13) on the shared datasource count?
- **B4. Gates P01Г?"P07 are all pending, and evidence items E03Г?"E10 don't exist.** The working tree is dirty. There is no historical source for the single `wallet_settings` row or for Binance minimum notional.
- **B5. The FIX-154 BUY path has no difference-register row.** `htfAuditStore` is optional-autowired into `TradeExecutionValidationService` (`:58`).
- **B6. The 99% acceptance method isn't frozen.** The confidence method, denominator, exclusion cap and sealed acceptance period are all missing (P07). Replay doesn't simulate fills, so a cost or slippage stance is out of scope, but that should be stated explicitly.

## OBJECTIONS
1. **Codex C4 overstated:** price ordering is correct in practice today; what's missing is the event id for evidence and logging. Wording should be corrected before freeze.
2. **Both agents:** the preparation calls itself "Replay only" (A1, A11.6) while assigning changes to shared classes (R156-05, R156-06). This contradiction has to be removed from the document; it can't be fixed during implementation.
3. **Both agents:** without one canonical, frozen copy of RPR-0.9.12 inside the repository, the decision-order reference, the D-rows and the fixture schema can't be audited.

## UNKNOWNS
- How Spring Boot 4.1 resolves the default executor for plain `@Async`.
- Whether `indicator_snapshot` records when each snapshot became available (needed for D54).
- How Replay reaches `shared_candle_input_audit` (D49).
- Whether historical `wallet_settings` values and Binance minimum notional can be reconstructed at all.
- The contents of RPR-0.9.12 itself. I didn't re-read or re-hash it.

## FINDINGS FOR JEAN
1. **Not freeze-ready.** Both agents reject it. The design goal (Replay matching Production non-wallet output) is achievable without touching Production source, but the document itself still assigns changes to two shared Production classes, `ExecutionReplayScope` and the price-event service.
2. **One decision unblocks most of the work:** copy the BUY logic into Replay, or run the unchanged Production BUY classes with Replay-supplied fixture settings and exchange limits. I recommend the second. The good news is that SELL validation doesn't touch wallet settings, so only the BUY path needs this.
3. **Two rulings are needed:**
   - Does redeploying the application to ship Replay-only code count as a Production change?
   - Do new Replay tables added by Flyway to the shared database count?
4. **Move RPR-0.9.12 into `agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`** so the "Section 3.3.1" exit-order rules are in the repository and can be reviewed and hashed.
5. **Smaller risks than I reported in Round 1:**
   - Price ordering is correct today; the gap is that no event id is recorded.
   - A new Replay thread pool won't change Production's `@Async` behaviour, provided it isn't named `taskExecutor`.
   - Regime state doesn't carry over in baseline runs.
   - These are now test requirements, not blockers.

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md =====
1: # Crypto AI Trader Г?" Architecture
2: 
3: Status labels used in this document:
4: 
5: - **Current** Г?" present in the repository today.
6: - **Pending** Г?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
7: - **Legacy/shared** Г?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
8:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
9: 
10: ## Current release status
11: 
12: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
13: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
14: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
15: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
16: 
17: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
18: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
19: 
20: **FIX-156 is in progress and Replay-only.** A foundation exists for immutable
21: Replay policy profiles, Replay-owned evidence/output tables, and a fail-closed
22: Replay SQL allow-list. The existing Replay service has not yet been rewired
23: away from its live wallet/Binance/shared-service dependencies, so FIX-156 is
24: not complete or deployable.
25: 
26: ## System purpose
27: 
28: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
29: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
30: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
31: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
32: 
33: ## Runtime and data ownership
34: 
35: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
36: |---|---|---|
37: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
38: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
39: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
40: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
41: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
42: 
43: ## Current primary flow
44: 
45: ```text
46: Binance WebSocket kline
47:   -> BinanceKlineService
48:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
49:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
50:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
51:       -> observer transaction
52:       -> close-dispatch transaction
53:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
54:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
55:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
56:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
57:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
58:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
59:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
60: ```
61: 
62: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
63: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
64: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
65: 
66: ## Current core persistence
67: 
68: | Table/domain | Purpose |
69: |---|---|
70: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
71: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
72: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
73: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
74: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
75: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
76: 
77: ## Legacy/shared-market path
78: 
79: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
80: 
81: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
82: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
83: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
84: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
85: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
86: 
87: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
88: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
89: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
90: 
91: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
92: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
93: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
94: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
95: 
96: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
97: 
98: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
99: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
100: path must never run together.
101: 
102: Implemented safeguards:
103: 
104: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
105:   closed candle.
106: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
107:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
108: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
109:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
110: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
111:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
112: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
113:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
114: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
115: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
116:   query retired shared-market tables.
117: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
118:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
119:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
120:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent

1: # Local candle coverage check Г?" 2026-10-09
2: 
3: - Scope: read-only check of all 21 enabled symbols in `crypto_ai.candle`, over the two-hour window ending at the database UTC clock.
4: - Current state: all 63 enabled symbol/interval lanes (1m, 5m, 1h) have closed-candle records and current live ingestion. The newest 1m candle was 54 seconds old at the check.
5: - Continuity: 5m and 1h had no internal gaps in the inspected window. Twelve symbols had one missing 1m candle: the interval from `2026-10-08T22:18:00Z` to `2026-10-08T22:19:00Z` (KSA `01:18` to `01:19`). This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.
6: - Impact: current live signals are being generated, but the missing historical 1m candles should be reconciled before relying on uninterrupted historical indicator lineage for that startup window. No database data or code was changed by this check.
7: 
8: # Signal-staleness root cause Г?" 2026-10-09
9: 
10: - System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
11: - The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
12: - Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
13: - `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
14: - 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
15: - No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.
16: 
17: ## Bounded local repair completed
18: 
19: - Authorized local repair finalized the 42 interrupted `RUNTIME` rows using Binance REST candles for the exact identities above: 21 x 1m and 21 x 5m.
20: - Each response was validated for the expected open and close timestamp before one transaction finalized only a row where `closed=0`. Existing closed candles, provenance, and close-transition tokens were not changed; no close-dispatch, analysis, signal, or trade was initiated by the repair.
21: - Verification: all 1m/5m 210-candle quality windows had zero gaps after the repair. The next live close produced new LIVE signals at `2026-10-09T07:30:07Z` for both 1m and 5m.
22: 
23: # ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation Г?" 2026-10-09
24: 
25: - Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
26: - Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
27: - Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
28: - Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
29: - Primary cause: the local candle-quality interruption stopped 1m ETH signals after 07:34 KSA. There were no ETH 1m signals from 07:34 to 10:26 KSA, the period containing most of the rise. The interrupted 1m and 5m rows were repaired at about 10:28 KSA, so analysis resumed only near the later consolidation/breakout attempt. This is a confirmed data-availability gap, not proof that the strategy would have bought near 07:54 KSA.
30: - Immediate signal sequence after recovery: at 10:30 KSA the 5m signal was a BUY but blocked by `RANGE_ENTRY_LOCATION`; at 10:31 KSA the 1m raw BUY was vetoed by a bearish BTC context; at 10:33 KSA BTC context became neutral and the 1m trend-following BUY was allowed. The entry signal itself explicitly recorded mixed higher-timeframe context (5m BUY, 1h NEUTRAL) and a weakening but ultra-close ask wall before its target.
31: - Assessment: the trade followed the implemented rules once local analysis resumed, and the trade was entered/exited promptly. It was nevertheless a late entry relative to the full 07:54Г?"10:33 KSA advance because the system lacked signal coverage during the main move. The data gap is an operational defect already covered by the local-candle recovery deployment gate. Whether the unobserved earlier period would have produced an executable BUY is uncertain without a controlled Replay run; none was started because that would create replay state.

36: - Timestamp correction (2026-10-10): the preceding interpretation was wrong
37:   because the read-only investigation connection used MySQL's `SYSTEM` timezone
38:   (KSA), while the application connection explicitly sets its session to UTC.

40:   `2026-10-09 23:45:06.682175`; converting that instant to UTC yields
41:   `2026-10-09 20:45:06.682175`. The Inspector's main trade card parses backend
42:   timestamps as UTC and renders them in the browser-local timezone. Therefore,
43:   with a KSA browser, its displayed 23:45 entry time is correct. Do not add
44:   three hours to the value shown by this KSA-session SQL connection.

50:   25%-size setup-wakeup route entered from a 5m BUY with 1h NEUTRAL context.

52:   the original TP distance. The dynamic lock activated at `0.000005421865` and
53:   live price `0.000005420` breached it. At the breach the current 1m and 5m
54:   were WATCH and 1h was NEUTRAL. The shared exit policy therefore returned
55:   `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`; it requires bullish 1h plus supportive 5m, or WATCH 1h
56:   plus supportive 5m, to hold a breached lock.
57: - TP extension: not evaluated. Extension begins only once price is at or above
58:   the original TP. The recorded high remained below TP at the time of exit.
59: - Subsequent action: later local candles reached `0.000005500` at about 00:40
60:   KSA, above the original TP. This is hindsight, not evidence available at the
61:   exit. Local SHIB 1m/5m coverage ends at about 00:56 KSA, so any claimed
62:   continuation through 12:15 KSA is not verifiable from local candle evidence.
63: - Assessment: behavior followed the existing protection rule, without a
64:   confirmed execution or latency defect. A future exit-policy change could
65:   explicitly allow a near-TP WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL continuation hold under
66:   measurable momentum/structure conditions and a tighter safety floor; it
67:   requires a separate design and Replay validation.

86: # SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10
87: 
88: - Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
89:   This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
90: - Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
91:   continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
92: - Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
93:   evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
94:   to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
95: - Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
96:   evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
97:   hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
98:   1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
99:   target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
100:   drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
101:   exit eligibility.
102: - Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
103:   or near-TP distance alone.
104: - Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
105:   with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
106:   1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
107:   protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
108:   lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
109: - Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
110:   paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
111:   comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
112:   gross profit is a net gain.
113: - Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
114:   unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
115:   near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
116:   calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
117:   pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
118:   qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
119:   a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
120:   momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10
2: 
3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
8:   capped at 25%.
9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
11:   never authorizes an add.
12: 
13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10
14: 
15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
16:   the data-quality gate.
17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
21:   generation after a repaired quality window.
===== codex-docs/CHANGELOG_AI.md =====
1: # 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 local-candle hardening status
2: 
3: - Implemented: OFF-mode trader-owned candle ingestion, monotonic close tokens,
4:   bounded detector/lanes, canonical price evidence, direct-attempt freshness,
5:   REST exchange-time close validation, and controlled ADMIN drain behavior.
6: - Operational impact: a successful drain exits the JVM; a timeout resumes
7:   admission. Price protection pauses during a drain window.
8: - Database impact: existing FIX-153 V106/B105 schema remains required; no
9:   production migration or data change was run by Codex.
10: - Release status: NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION. Java 21, disposable-MySQL,
11:   crash/race, Windows lock, soak and Replay-parity validation remain open.
12: 
13: # 2026-10-08 Г?" FIX-153 exchange/database clock correction
14: 
15: - Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
16:   too early or too old during freshness checks.
17: - Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
18:   database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
19:   callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
20: - The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
21:   dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
22: - General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
23:   scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
24: - Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
25:   the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.
26: 
27: # 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 audit: analysis outcomes and protection clock
28: 
29: - Audit result: normal live and historical analysis persist a `TradeSignal`,
30:   including neutral/no-entry decisions; there is no normal analysed-without-
31:   durable-outcome path to reconcile.
32: - Corrected the post-lock direct-protection timestamp validation to use the
33:   JVM-to-exchange clock correction, matching initial receipt validation.
34: - A universal shutdown barrier remains a separate, unverified design task:
35:   it must cover financial mutations without treating unrelated administration
36:   or configuration writes as trading authority.
37: - Database impact: none. Production and Replay impact: execution freshness now
38:   follows an automatically measured exchange-aligned reference clock.
39: - Validation still required: unavailable-time-source, stale observation,
40:   positive/negative skew, and first-attempt freshness regression tests.
41: 
42: # 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-154 identifier alignment
43: 
44: - The pending higher-timeframe BUY-validation design was renumbered from
45:   `FIX-146` to `FIX-154` in the project guidance and architecture record.
46: - It remains separate from FIX-153, unimplemented, and not approved for
47:   deployment. There is no database, Production, or Replay behavior change.
48: 
49: # 2026-10-09 Г?" Automated local-candle reconciliation direction
50: 
51: - Approved direction: the pending local-candle reconciler must automatically
52:   repair authoritative interrupted-open candles and exact absent identities in
53:   the active quality window. Inserted identities use `BACKFILL` provenance and
54:   never receive a close token or dispatch live analysis.
55: - No manual approval, database repair, or persisted review fence may be needed
56:   for pending candle/analysis reconciliation. Unverified data remains explicit
57:   non-executable evidence; this direction does not override legitimate
58:   strategy or risk-entry vetoes.
59: - Status: design only; no database migration, code, Production, or Replay
60:   behavior has changed.
61: 
62: # 2026-10-09 Г?" Appendix K reconciliation contract alignment
63: 
64: - Project guidance now records the approved pending design constraints: pure
65:   insert-only authoritative gap backfill; quality-window-bounded candidates;
66:   durable terminal outcomes; structurally isolated historical processing; a
67:   shared Binance REST rate-limit gate; and durable financial-effect
68:   reconciliation only after atomicity is proven.
69: - The planned universal mutation barrier is explicitly in-memory and active
70:   only during drain; it must not queue, delay, or persistently fence fresh
71:   trading outside drain. Protection for open positions during replacement is
72:   still an unresolved release gate.
73: - Status: documentation/design alignment only. No runtime behavior, schema,
74:   Production data, or Replay behavior was changed.
75: 
76: # 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 automatic reconciliation implementation (initial slice)
77: 
78: - Problem: reconnect-boundary or startup candle gaps could leave local history
79:   invalid until a person performed a database repair.
80: - Solution: added V107 durable reconciliation/audit and terminal-outcome
81:   tables; guarded update-only authoritative recovery; pure insert-only gap
82:   backfill; a dedicated recovery executor; reconnect/scheduled requests;
83:   token-free recovery outcomes; post-ingestion late-live-close observation;
84:   and reconciliation participation in local drain.
85: - Files/classes changed: `CandleRepository`, `BinanceMarketDataClient`,
86:   `BinanceKlineService`, `BinanceWebSocketManager`, `LocalDrainService`,
87:   `LocalCandleHealth`, `SignalProcessingStore`, plus new local reconciliation,
88:   outcome, audit, executor-config, and V107 migration files.
89: - Database impact: forward migration V107 is required. Production and Replay
90:   impact: feature is disabled by default and has not been deployed. Universal
91:   mutation-barrier coverage, shared REST admission/rate-limit control,
92:   quarantined-effect reconciliation, MySQL crash/race tests, and Replay parity
93:   remain open release work.
94: 
95: # 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 reconciliation outcome correction
96: 
97: - Corrected recovery semantics so `SKIPPED_NOT_LIVE` is recorded only when a
98:   recovery/backfill actually changes candle evidence; a competing live or
99:   existing row is not relabelled.
100: - `REST_NOT_FINAL` is now retryable rather than a terminal mismatch. Audit keys
101:   use fixed epoch-millisecond identity precision, and reconciliation is safely
102:   disabled by default even when configuration is absent.
103: - Added terminal outcomes for worker data-unavailable, historical-only,
104:   already-analysed, stale work, and detector expiry paths. Transient failures
105:   remain without a terminal outcome so retry remains possible.
106: - Added `LocalReconciliationAuditRepositoryTest`; targeted Java 21 tests and
107:   compile passed. MySQL, race/crash, rate-limit, barrier, effect-reconciler,
108:   and Replay validation remain open.
109: 
110: # 2026-10-09 Г?" FIX-153 reconciliation safety correction
111: 
112: - Recovery now requires positive loss evidence before it may finalise an open
113:   row: either the successor candle is already present or a reconnect was
114:   admitted after the candidate close. This prevents a short healthy-stream
115:   delay from being converted into a token-free, unanalysed close.
116: - REST finality/identity disagreement is retryable while the local row remains
117:   open. The authoritative Binance close timestamp, not the local expected
118:   value, is persisted after a successful exact match. A bounded first-candle
119:   probe identifies pre-listing identities as explicit unavailable outcomes.
120: - Recovery uses the short timeout client and checks drain between requests;
===== agent-docs/FIX-156_IMPLEMENTATION_PREPARATION.md =====
1: # FIX-156 - Replay/Production Parity: Consolidated Preparation and Final Pre-Implementation Review
2: 
3: **Revision:** Consolidated preparation, E01/E02 draft evidence, and final static code review, 2026-10-15  
4: **Status:** NOT APPROVED FOR IMPLEMENTATION  
5: **Prepared:** 2026-10-11 KSA  
6: **Source design:** owner-provided `FIX-156-RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT.md`  
7: **RPR-0.9.12 SHA-256:** `ddfd5f64a95f6b55ed39e8bffa7b4c93bd65f4e6dd3b23d8bf821500de722b24`  
8: **Owner decision O-8:** RPR-0.9.12 incorporates option (a), the narrowly scoped `fix122_evaluation` exception in its Section 2.1 and in D48/R156-05/T156-09b.  
9: **This document review SHA-256:** Pending final freeze; calculate and record it in the review request only after the last content edit.  
10: **Application/configuration/migration/database/service/deployment changes made by this document:** NONE
11: 
12: ## 1. Fixed scope and earned results
13: 
14: FIX-156 changes **Replay only**. It does not change Production source,
15: behaviour, database writes, account state, schedulers, HTTP traffic, or
16: latency. Replay is a manual single-symbol research tool with fixed notional
17: output. It is not a wallet, balance, cash, reserve, budget, portfolio, or
18: historical-fill simulator.
19: 
20: A run has two independent earned certification dimensions:
21: 
22: - `ANALYSIS_PARITY`: a qualified Production signal/outcome is reproduced
23:   using as-of evidence.
24: - `LIFECYCLE_PARITY`: a qualified recorded-entry lifecycle passes the
25:   separate lifecycle metric and critical-safety gate.
26: 
27: A run may earn neither, one, or both dimensions. `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`
28: and `FAILED_REPLAY` are terminal states which prevent either claim.
29: `FAILED_REPLAY` is only for a technical error, cancellation, or failed
30: staging. A business mismatch is published as parity-not-earned with its first
31: divergence; it is not a technical failure.
32: 
33: No result may claim wallet, account, exact-fill, live-scheduling, MySQL-lock,
34: or Binance-availability parity.
35: 
36: ## 2. Decided owner rules
37: 
38: | Decision | Implementable rule |
39: | --- | --- |
40: | O-1 | Replay never calls `wallet_settings` or shared `validateBuy`. A Replay-local BUY mirror reads only a frozen policy profile. |
41: | O-2 | Signal and lifecycle parity are separate certification dimensions. |
42: | O-3 | Every class reachable from a Replay request or worker is inside the boundary. |
43: | O-4 | The 99% result is separate per metric, based on a frozen lower confidence bound, coverage floor, and critical-safety gate. |
44: | O-5 | A lifecycle fixture carries the full recorded timeline, not entry only. |
45: | O-6 | No separate Replay database identity/datasource is introduced. |
46: | O-7 | No shared-datasource SQL guard is introduced. Replay safety is application/test enforced, not a database-permission claim. |
47: | O-8 / D48 | Replay may use `fix122_evaluation` only through the exact run-owned and display-only operations in Section 4.2a. This exception never supplies decision, wallet, account, fill, or Production-trading authority. |
48:

83: 
84: ### 4.2a `fix122_evaluation` transitional shared-table exception and delete policy
85: 
86: `fix122_evaluation` is a shared diagnostics/evidence table which Production
87: also writes. The approved D48 boundary exception is deliberately narrow: it
88: supports run-owned Replay diagnostic evidence plus one read-only Production
89: diagnostic display. It cannot participate in a Replay decision, parity
90: classification, qualification, entry, exit, sizing, or lifecycle transition.
91: 
92: The permitted run-owned operations are:
93: 
94: ```sql
95: DELETE FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NOT NULL
96: ```
97: 
98: For the eventual baseline, this is an explicit table allow-list exception, not
99: an untracked boundary violation. The allow-list and static reachability tests
100: must permit only the following exact operations:
101: 
102: - read, insert, and query rows whose `test_run_id` identifies the active
103:   Replay run;
104: - the scoped purge above, never an unqualified delete and never a delete of a
105:   row where `test_run_id IS NULL`;
106: - the existing read-only display query `SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE
107:   test_run_id IS NULL AND symbol=? AND evaluated_at>=? AND evaluated_at<=?
108:   ORDER BY id`. It may populate diagnostics display only and must not feed a
109:   decision, parity result, fixture, or any mutable state;
110: - no other select, insert, update, delete, repository, JDBC template, or
111:   indirect access to this table.
112: 
113: The direct and background-task tests must fail the build for any operation
114: outside this exact policy. Any other shared-table access remains forbidden.
115: D48 remains open until the implemented policy and its direct/background-task
116: tests prove these limits. RPR-0.9.12 adopts this owner decision. It remains a
117: draft and is not an implementation authorization.
118: 
119: ## 5. Retirements from the baseline only
120: 
===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md =====
1: # Replay / Production Business-Logic-Parity Redesign
2: 
3: **Revision:** RPR-0.4-DRAFT  
4: **Status:** DRAFT Г?" NOT APPROVED Г?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
5: **Created:** 2026-10-10  
6: **Scope owner:** Codex implementation / Claude independent review
7: 
8: ## 1. Business objective
9: 
10: Make manual Replay reproduce the current paper-trading Production business flow
11: as closely as durable historical evidence permits, while remaining completely
12: isolated from Production trading state and wallet data.
13: 
14: The exact intended flow is:
15: 
16: ```text
17: historical candle + historical context + canonical accepted price events
18:   -> shared Production indicator/signal/decision policies
19:   -> replay-local virtual wallet/account state
20:   -> shared sizing/allocation/entry revalidation policies
21:   -> replay-local position lifecycle
22:   -> shared TP/SL/profit-lock/continuation/exit policies
23:   -> replay output + explicit parity certificate
24: ```
25: 
26: Replay must never write `wallet_*`, `paper_position`, `trade_signal`,
27: `signal_processing_work`, or any other Production trading table.
28: 
29: The result is not a claim that historical Java scheduling, MySQL lock timing,
30: network delay, external Binance availability, or the historical Production
31: wallet balance can be reproduced. It is a verified **business-logic parity**
32: claim only when all required market/decision evidence exists. Otherwise Replay
33: remains available as a labelled simulation and may not claim parity.
34: 
35: ## 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence
36: 
37: The 2026-10-10 source audit established the following:
38: 
39: 1. Signal, entry, normal-exit, continuation, profit-lock, and near-TP policy
40:    use several shared Production services.
41: 2. `ShadowProductionReplayService` separately owns replay wallet cash,
42:    positions, daily budget, persistence, price-loop lifecycle, and result
43:    labels. It starts at `INITIAL_CAPITAL=10000`, runs one symbol/position, and
44:    does not reproduce Production wallet/global controls.
45: 3. A run is labelled `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` when at least one accepted price
46:    event exists, rather than proving coverage for every interval while a
47:    position was open.
48: 4. Historical derivatives information is not persisted;
49:    `DerivativesPositioningService.evaluateHistorical()` returns UNAVAILABLE.
50:    Historical order-book evaluation can likewise be unavailable when snapshots
51:    were not retained.
52: 5. Replay-only FIX-11K and FIX-11Q observers, and the pre-FIX-122 UI revision,
53:    have no current Production equivalent and must not contaminate parity runs.
54: 6. Current Replay reads `WalletSettingsRepository` directly and its near-TP
55:    path can call `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy`, which can request a current
56:    live Binance minimum notional. Both violate replay account/external-input
57:    isolation.
58: 
59: ## 3. Boundaries
60: 
61: ### In scope
62: 
63: - Replay input/evidence qualification and run-level parity status.
64: - Replay-local virtual wallet state, wallet controls, positions, executions,
65:   daily statistics, and global limit reproduction.
66: - Canonical price-event coverage qualification and event ordering.
67: - Historical context-evidence qualification for derivatives and order book.
68: - Removal of approved retired Replay-only business/experiment behaviour.
69: - Parity certificate, diagnostics, migrations, tests, documentation.
70: 
71: ### Out of scope
72: 
73: - Any Production strategy change, sizing change, exit change, or wallet change.
74: - Live-exchange orders, fills, fees, slippage, or order-book execution modelling.
75: - Replaying database locks, thread scheduling, JVM pauses, WebSocket transport,
76:   external REST failures, or transaction retries as historical facts.
77: - FIX-153, FIX-154, and FIX-155 trading-policy changes.
78: - Deleting Production evidence or existing historical Replay/archive rows.
79: 
80: ## 4. Contractual modes
81: 
82: | Mode | Purpose | Missing evidence behaviour | Claim allowed |
83: |---|---|---|---|
84: | `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` | Reproduce Production rules using an explicitly selected virtual Replay account and complete market/decision evidence. | Refuse the run before execution. | Business-logic parity. |
85: | `SIMULATION` | Manual what-if/research run. | Continue but persist all approximation reasons. | Simulation only. |
86: | `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` | Explicit, separately approved research such as FIX-155. | Per experiment contract. | Never Production parity. |
87: 
88: The default Administration Replay action must be `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY`; the UI
89: must make `SIMULATION` and `EXPERIMENTAL_RESEARCH` explicit opt-in choices.
90: 
91: ## 5. Business-logic-parity eligibility
92: 
93: A `BUSINESS_LOGIC_PARITY` run must fail before generated signals or shadow wallet
94: state are written unless all of the following hold:
95: 
96: 1. Every requested candle identity is closed and within the quality contract.
97: 2. A complete immutable `ReplayAccountProfile` is supplied: starting virtual
98:    USDT, reserve, daily budget, daily loss state, maximum-new-position/global
99:    limits, and wallet settings. It is copied to replay-only tables at run start
100:    and is never read from or written to a Production wallet table.
101: 3. The selected replay universe is declared. The virtual account applies
102:    global limits across every symbol included in that run; no claim is made
103:    that it reproduces the historical Production wallet's other positions.
104: 4. The selected profile contains versioned execution constraints for relevant
105:    symbols, including minimum executable notional. Replay never fetches a
106:    current exchange constraint while evaluating historical events.
107: 5. Every canonical direct price observation required from the first replay
108:    position open through its terminal close is represented by accepted
109:    `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` evidence, with no unresolved `PENDING` row in the whole
110:    consumed range.
111: 6. Required historical order-book evidence exists whenever the feature was
112:    enabled for the corresponding Production decision.
113: 7. Required historical derivatives evidence exists whenever derivatives were
114:    enabled and could change confidence or entry authority. Until durable
115:    derivatives persistence exists, affected requests must be refused in this
116:    mode rather than silently treating UNAVAILABLE as equivalent.
117: 8. The exact Production configuration/version fingerprint is available for the
118:    requested period, or all version-dependent decision paths are marked
119:    unavailable and the mode is refused.
120:

1: # FIX-156 - Replay/Production Parity: Consolidated Preparation and Final Pre-Implementation Review
2: 
3: **Revision:** Consolidated preparation, E01/E02 draft evidence, and final static code review, 2026-10-15  
4: **Status:** NOT APPROVED FOR IMPLEMENTATION  
5: **Prepared:** 2026-10-11 KSA  
6: **Source design:** owner-provided `FIX-156-RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT.md`  
7: **RPR-0.9.12 SHA-256:** `ddfd5f64a95f6b55ed39e8bffa7b4c93bd65f4e6dd3b23d8bf821500de722b24`  
8: **Owner decision O-8:** RPR-0.9.12 incorporates option (a), the narrowly scoped `fix122_evaluation` exception in its Section 2.1 and in D48/R156-05/T156-09b.  
9: **This document review SHA-256:** Pending final freeze; calculate and record it in the review request only after the last content edit.  
10: **Application/configuration/migration/database/service/deployment changes made by this document:** NONE
11: 
12: ## 1. Fixed scope and earned results
13: 
14: FIX-156 changes **Replay only**. It does not change Production source,
15: behaviour, database writes, account state, schedulers, HTTP traffic, or
16: latency. Replay is a manual single-symbol research tool with fixed notional
17: output. It is not a wallet, balance, cash, reserve, budget, portfolio, or
18: historical-fill simulator.
19: 
20: A run has two independent earned certification dimensions:
21: 
22: - `ANALYSIS_PARITY`: a qualified Production signal/outcome is reproduced
23:   using as-of evidence.
24: - `LIFECYCLE_PARITY`: a qualified recorded-entry lifecycle passes the
25:   separate lifecycle metric and critical-safety gate.
26: 
27: A run may earn neither, one, or both dimensions. `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`
28: and `FAILED_REPLAY` are terminal states which prevent either claim.
29: `FAILED_REPLAY` is only for a technical error, cancellation, or failed
30: staging. A business mismatch is published as parity-not-earned with its first
31: divergence; it is not a technical failure.
32: 
33: No result may claim wallet, account, exact-fill, live-scheduling, MySQL-lock,
34: or Binance-availability parity.
35: 
36: ## 2. Decided owner rules
37: 
38: | Decision | Implementable rule |
39: | --- | --- |
40: | O-1 | Replay never calls `wallet_settings` or shared `validateBuy`. A Replay-local BUY mirror reads only a frozen policy profile. |
41: | O-2 | Signal and lifecycle parity are separate certification dimensions. |
42: | O-3 | Every class reachable from a Replay request or worker is inside the boundary. |
43: | O-4 | The 99% result is separate per metric, based on a frozen lower confidence bound, coverage floor, and critical-safety gate. |
44: | O-5 | A lifecycle fixture carries the full recorded timeline, not entry only. |
45: | O-6 | No separate Replay database identity/datasource is introduced. |
46: | O-7 | No shared-datasource SQL guard is introduced. Replay safety is application/test enforced, not a database-permission claim. |
47: | O-8 / D48 | Replay may use `fix122_evaluation` only through the exact run-owned and display-only operations in Section 4.2a. This exception never supplies decision, wallet, account, fill, or Production-trading authority. |

84: ### 4.2a `fix122_evaluation` transitional shared-table exception and delete policy
85: 
86: `fix122_evaluation` is a shared diagnostics/evidence table which Production
87: also writes. The approved D48 boundary exception is deliberately narrow: it
88: supports run-owned Replay diagnostic evidence plus one read-only Production
89: diagnostic display. It cannot participate in a Replay decision, parity
90: classification, qualification, entry, exit, sizing, or lifecycle transition.
91: 
92: The permitted run-owned operations are:
93: 
94: ```sql
95: DELETE FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE test_run_id IS NOT NULL
96: ```
97: 
98: For the eventual baseline, this is an explicit table allow-list exception, not
99: an untracked boundary violation. The allow-list and static reachability tests
100: must permit only the following exact operations:
101: 
102: - read, insert, and query rows whose `test_run_id` identifies the active
103:   Replay run;
104: - the scoped purge above, never an unqualified delete and never a delete of a
105:   row where `test_run_id IS NULL`;
106: - the existing read-only display query `SELECT * FROM fix122_evaluation WHERE
107:   test_run_id IS NULL AND symbol=? AND evaluated_at>=? AND evaluated_at<=?
108:   ORDER BY id`. It may populate diagnostics display only and must not feed a
109:   decision, parity result, fixture, or any mutable state;
110: - no other select, insert, update, delete, repository, JDBC template, or
111:   indirect access to this table.
112: 
113: The direct and background-task tests must fail the build for any operation
114: outside this exact policy. Any other shared-table access remains forbidden.
115: D48 remains open until the implemented policy and its direct/background-task
116: tests prove these limits. RPR-0.9.12 adopts this owner decision. It remains a
117: draft and is not an implementation authorization.

132: ## 6. Mandatory logging registry
133: 
134: There is one registry only. Every Replay log starts
135: `[FIX-156][REPLAY]`, includes `runId`, `profileHash`, `baselineHash`,
136: `symbol`, and when present `fixtureId`, `signalId`, and `priceEventId`.
137: Every time is displayed as stored UTC **and** KSA (`+03:00`). No credential,
138: token, account number, raw SQL parameter, or unbounded payload is logged.
139: 
140: The only event IDs are:
141: 
142: - `RUN_REQUESTED`, `RUN_QUALIFIED`, `RUN_REFUSED`, `RUN_FAILED`, `RUN_PROMOTED`;
143: - `FIXTURE_LOADED`;
144: - `PRICE_SPAN`;
145: - `DECISION`;
146: - `FIRST_DIVERGENCE`;
147: - `FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY`; and
148: - `POLICY_FINGERPRINT`.
149: 
150: Event payloads include the RPR-0.9.10 required fields: reason/evidence counts,
151: fixture hash/count, price-span boundaries/gap/refusal, decision path and
152: Section-3.3.1 step, input event IDs, expected/actual first divergence, attempted
153: class/method/table/URL, and policy source/configuration/profile identity.
154: Volume is bounded by E05. Tests assert one correctly correlated event per
155: occurrence.
156: 
157: A critical-safety mismatch is `ERROR` and **parity-not-earned**. Its mismatch
158: certificate and first divergence are published. It never becomes
159: `FAILED_REPLAY` merely because the result disagrees. `FAILED_REPLAY` means
160: technical error/cancellation/failed staging and discards staged artifacts.
161:

196: all required tests and E01-E10 evidence must exist; the qualified/excluded/
197: refused/non-comparable/censored buckets must be reported by cause and exit

199: separately for both dimensions.

202: profit-lock, take-profit, near-TP ownership and duplicate-exit classes. A

204: `LIFECYCLE_PARITY` dimension only. It never earns account or exact-fill parity.

283: | D45 / N16 | 1m wake-up handling differs internally but appears decision-equivalent. | Prove equivalent fixture. |
284: | D46 / N17 | Rebase-floor arithmetic uses different precision policies. | Numeric tolerance fixture. |
285: 
286: ### Consequence for approval gates
287: 
288: P02 and P03 remain pending. E01/E02 identify scope and register changes but do
289: not close E03-E10, do not prove runtime behaviour, and do not authorise a
290: Replay implementation. RPR-0.9.12-DRAFT must now be reviewed and frozen with
291: the required source/configuration/migration/worktree evidence before approval.
292:

297: - No application or Replay configuration was changed.
298: - No database was queried, written, repaired, or migrated.
299: - No service was started/stopped and no deployment action occurred.

386: 4. Update the preparation registry only where that completed evidence requires it, while keeping RPR-0.9.12 as the canonical contract.
387: 5. Obtain explicit user authorization for the exact reviewed and frozen revision.
388: 
389: The recorded P01 status reports a large dirty working tree. It cannot serve
390: as a reproducible baseline until the hashes and a clean baseline are
391: recorded.
392: 
393: ### 11.6 Implementation boundary after authorization
394: 
395: FIX-156 may change Replay-owned code, fixtures, isolated Replay tables,
396: tests, and documentation only. It must not change Production trading logic,
397: Production wallet behaviour, Production database records, live Binance
398: behaviour, or deployment configuration.
399: 
400: The future implementation must include the registered structured logs with
401: UTC and KSA values, distinguish `ANALYSIS_PARITY` from `LIFECYCLE_PARITY`,
402: treat a business mismatch as parity-not-earned rather than `FAILED_REPLAY`,
403: and reject a parity claim for a critical-safety mismatch.

544:                         runId, current.positionId(), symbol, minimumCheck.code(), soldQty,
545:                         minimumCheck.requestedNotional(), minimumCheck.minimumNotional());

547:             }

549:             BigDecimal soldCost = current.entryPrice().multiply(soldQty, MC);
550:             BigDecimal partialPnl = proceeds.subtract(soldCost, MC);

652:         // 1m signal TradeExecutionValidationService. Replay executes both shared production
653:         // policies in the same order instead of carrying a test-only SELL rule.

663:             }

668:     private FallbackNearTpEvaluation evaluateNearTpAtSignalFallback(long runId, ShadowPosition current,
669:                                                                     TradeSignal signal, TradeSignal oneMinute,
670:                                                                     TradeSignal fiveMinute) {
671:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State state = new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
672:                 current.nearTpState(), current.nearTpBestPrice(), current.nearTpBearishStreak(),
673:                 current.nearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), current.nearTpHarvestUsed());
674:         NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.Evaluation evaluation = nearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.evaluate(
675:                 state, current.entryPrice(), current.takeProfit(), signal.getLatestPrice(), signal.getGeneratedAt(),
676:                 oneMinute, fiveMinute);
677:         ShadowPosition updated = current.withNearTp(evaluation.state());
678:         if (!updated.sameNearTp(current)) persistNearTpState(updated);
679:         if (evaluation.transition()) {
680:             log.info("FIX-11T Replay fallback Near-TP: runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, code={}, state={}, price={}, givebackPct={}, bearish1mStreak={}, detail={}",
681:                     runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), evaluation.code(), updated.nearTpState(),
682:                     signal.getLatestPrice(), evaluation.givebackPercent(), updated.nearTpBearishStreak(), evaluation.explanation());
683:         }
684:         if (!evaluation.harvestEligible()) return new FallbackNearTpEvaluation(updated, BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal.ZERO);
685: 
686:         BigDecimal soldQty = updated.quantity().multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(0.50), MC);
687:         BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.Evaluation minimumCheck =
688:                 binanceMinimumExecutionPolicy.evaluate(signal.getSymbol(), soldQty, signal.getLatestPrice());
689:         if (!minimumCheck.executable()) {
690:             updated = updated.withNearTp(new NearTpFailureProtectionPolicy.State(
691:                     NearTpState.NEAR_TP_REJECTION_DETECTED, updated.nearTpBestPrice(),
692:                     0, updated.nearTpLastOneMinuteSignalId(), false));
693:             persistNearTpState(updated);
694:             log.info("FIX-11T Replay fallback NEAR_TP_HARVEST_SKIPPED: runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, reason={}, requestedQty={}, requestedNotional={}, binanceMinimum={}; existing position management continues",
695:                     runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), minimumCheck.code(), soldQty,
696:                     minimumCheck.requestedNotional(), minimumCheck.minimumNotional());
697:             return new FallbackNearTpEvaluation(updated, BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal.ZERO);
698:         }
699:         BigDecimal proceeds = soldQty.multiply(signal.getLatestPrice(), MC);
700:         BigDecimal soldCost = updated.entryPrice().multiply(soldQty, MC);
701:         BigDecimal pnl = proceeds.subtract(soldCost, MC);
702:         BigDecimal pnlPct = soldCost.signum() == 0 ? BigDecimal.ZERO : pnl.divide(soldCost, 8, RoundingMode.HALF_UP)
703:                 .multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100));
704:         updated = updated.withPartialHarvest(soldQty, soldCost, pnl);
705:         persistNearTpPartialHarvestAtSignal(runId, signal, updated, soldQty, proceeds, pnl, pnlPct, evaluation.explanation());
706:         log.info("FIX-11T Replay fallback NEAR_TP_PARTIAL_HARVESTED: runId={}, positionId={}, symbol={}, soldQty={}, remainingQty={}, price={}, partialPnl={}",
707:                 runId, updated.positionId(), signal.getSymbol(), soldQty, updated.quantity(), signal.getLatestPrice(), pnl);
708:         return new FallbackNearTpEvaluation(updated, proceeds, pnl);
709:     }

711:     private ShadowPosition updateProfitLock(ShadowPosition p, TradeSignal s) {
712:         if (s.getLatestPrice() == null || !priceAuthorityPolicy.canUseSignalPrice(s, p.entryTime())) return p;
713:         ShadowPosition n = profitLockState(p, s.getLatestPrice());
714:         if (!equalsNullable(p.highest(), n.highest()) || p.profitLockActive() != n.profitLockActive()
715:                 || !equalsNullable(p.profitLockPrice(), n.profitLockPrice())
716:                 || p.profitLockState() != n.profitLockState()
717:                 || !java.util.Objects.equals(p.profitLockRebaseStartedAt(), n.profitLockRebaseStartedAt())) {
718:             jdbcTemplate.update("UPDATE wallet_position_test SET highest_price_usdt=?, profit_lock_active=?, profit_lock_price_usdt=?, profit_lock_state=?, profit_lock_rebase_started_at=? WHERE id=?",
719:                     n.highest(), n.profitLockActive(), n.profitLockPrice(), n.profitLockState().name(),
720:                     n.profitLockRebaseStartedAt() == null ? null : Timestamp.from(n.profitLockRebaseStartedAt()), p.positionId());
721:         }
722:         return n;

725:     private ShadowPosition profitLockState(ShadowPosition p, BigDecimal price) {
726:         WalletSettings settings = walletSettings();
727:         BigDecimal activation = nvl(settings.getProfitLockActivationPercent(), BigDecimal.valueOf(70));
728:         BigDecimal highest = p.highest() == null ? price : p.highest().max(price);
729:         BigDecimal progress = progressPercent(p.entryPrice(), p.takeProfit(), highest);
730: 
731:         if (p.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
732:                 && progress.compareTo(activation) < 0) {
733:             // Replay parity with Production FIX-118: preserve the historical lock/legacy flag,
734:             // but keep it non-executable until the new TP geometry earns activation again.
735:             return p.withLock(highest, true, p.profitLockPrice(), ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE,
736:                     p.profitLockRebaseStartedAt());
737:         }
738: 
739:         // FIX-118 parity: when REBASE earns activation, rebuild the lock from the current
740:         // TP geometry instead of retaining the superseded pre-extension lock.
741:         boolean freshGeometryActivation = p.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE;
742:         ProfitLockPolicy.State state = profitLockPolicy.evaluate(
743:                 p.entryPrice(), p.takeProfit(), price, highest,
744:                 freshGeometryActivation ? false : (p.profitLockState() == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE || p.profitLockActive()),
745:                 freshGeometryActivation ? null : p.profitLockPrice(),
746:                 settings.isDynamicProfitLockEnabled(), activation,
747:                 nvl(settings.getProfitLockInitialPercent(), BigDecimal.valueOf(40)),
748:                 nvl(settings.getProfitLockTrailStepPercent(), BigDecimal.valueOf(10)));
749:         ProfitLockState nextState = state.active() ? ProfitLockState.ACTIVE : ProfitLockState.INACTIVE;
750:         return p.withLock(state.highestPrice(), state.active(), state.lockPrice(), nextState, null);
751:     }

1018:     private BigDecimal totalPositionPnlPercent(ShadowPosition position, BigDecimal remainingLegPnl) {
1019:         BigDecimal totalPnl = position.partialRealizedPnl().add(remainingLegPnl, MC);
1020:         BigDecimal totalInvestedCost = position.cost().add(position.partialHarvestCostBasis(), MC);
1021:         return totalInvestedCost.signum() == 0 ? BigDecimal.ZERO
1022:                 : totalPnl.divide(totalInvestedCost, 8, RoundingMode.HALF_UP).multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(100));
1023:     }

106:     public List<TradeSignal> recent(String symbol, String interval, Instant reference, int limit) {
107:         State s = required();

69:             // FIX-112A: a bullish label alone is not a consumed entry. Only keep

71:             // executed BUY (Production) or executed shadow BUY (Replay).

73:                     && isBullish(previous.getDecision())
74:                     && entryConsumptionPolicy.resolve(previous.getId()) != EntryConsumptionState.NOT_CONSUMED) {
75:                 return ValidationResult.reject("BUY_CONTINUATION",

84:                     "No fresh 5m confirmation was available for the 1m BUY signal.");

385:                 && !signal.getGeneratedAt().isBefore(reference.minus(maximumAge));

413:     private ValidationResult cap(ValidationResult result, HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment) {
414:         if (!result.allowed() || assessment.maxInitialPercent()==null) return result;
415:         int capped=Math.min(result.positionPercent(),assessment.maxInitialPercent());
416:         if (capped != result.positionPercent() && fix154Metrics != null) fix154Metrics.softCappedEntry();
417:         return capped==result.positionPercent()?result:ValidationResult.allow(capped,result.code(),result.explanation()+" FIX-154 soft-HTF cap="+capped+"%.");
418:     }

420:     /** Used only after progressive raw-HTF checks have already failed; never changes an add decision. */
421:     public void recordProgressiveAssessmentIfSoftBlocked(TradeSignal referenceSignal) {
422:         if (referenceSignal == null || referenceSignal.getGeneratedAt() == null) return;
423:         TradeSignal five = latestAtOrBefore(referenceSignal, CONFIRMATION_INTERVAL);
424:         TradeSignal one = latestAtOrBefore(referenceSignal, TREND_INTERVAL);
425:         if (!isFresh(five, referenceSignal.getGeneratedAt(), FIVE_MINUTE_MAX_AGE)
426:                 || !isFresh(one, referenceSignal.getGeneratedAt(), ONE_HOUR_MAX_AGE)) return;
427:         HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment = htfAssessment.assess(five, true, one, true);
428:         if (assessment.band() == HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Band.SOFT_CONFLICT) {
429:             if (fix154Metrics != null) fix154Metrics.softAddBlocked();
430:             audit(referenceSignal, "PROGRESSIVE_ADD", assessment, "SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED",
431:                     "Existing raw 1h progressive-add guard blocked this add; FIX-154 audit only.");
432:         }
433:     }

435:     private void audit(TradeSignal referenceSignal, String path,
436:                        HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService.Assessment assessment, String finalResult, String reason) {
437:         if (htfAuditStore == null) return;
438:         try {
439:             htfAuditStore.record(referenceSignal, path, assessment, finalResult, reason);
440:         } catch (RuntimeException auditFailure) {
441:             if (fix154Metrics != null) fix154Metrics.auditWriteFailed();
442:             log.warn("FIX-154 audit handoff failed; BUY decision remains unchanged: path={}, signalId={}",
443:                     path, referenceSignal == null ? null : referenceSignal.getId(), auditFailure);
444:         }
445:     }

1: package com.crypto.execution.service;
2: 
3: import com.crypto.execution.domain.EntryConsumptionState;
4: import com.crypto.wallet.repository.WalletTradeRepository;
5: import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
6: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
7: 
8: /**
9:  * FIX-112A: consumption belongs to the specific bullish signal, not to the
10:  * symbol's general position state. A pre-existing position can coexist with a
11:  * blocked progressive-add signal, so OPEN-position state is not proof that the
12:  * immediately previous bullish signal actually executed.
13:  */
14: @Service
15: @RequiredArgsConstructor
16: public class EntryConsumptionPolicy {
17:     private final WalletTradeRepository walletTradeRepository;
18:     private final ExecutionReplayScope replayScope;
19: 
20:     public EntryConsumptionState resolve(Long previousSignalId) {
21:         if (previousSignalId == null) return EntryConsumptionState.NOT_CONSUMED;
22:         if (replayScope.active()) {
23:             return replayScope.entryConsumed(previousSignalId)
24:                     ? EntryConsumptionState.CONSUMED
25:                     : EntryConsumptionState.NOT_CONSUMED;
26:         }
27:         // Production authority is the immutable executed BUY row keyed to the
28:         // exact triggering signal. This works for both first entries and adds.
29:         boolean executed = walletTradeRepository
30:                 .findTopBySignalIdAndSideAndStatusOrderByExecutedAtDesc(
31:                         previousSignalId, "BUY", "EXECUTED")
32:                 .isPresent();
33:         return executed ? EntryConsumptionState.CONSUMED : EntryConsumptionState.NOT_CONSUMED;
34:     }
35: }
340:         }
341: 
342:         // FIX-088: Delete Data is now a true purge, not an archive operation. Every replay/test
343:         // table that can feed Recent Test Runs, run detail, Shadow Trades, or archived replay
344:         // history is emptied. Persistent Proven records are deliberately excluded.

-- FIX-156: additive Replay-owned evidence only; no existing table is altered.
CREATE TABLE replay_policy_profile (
    run_id BIGINT NOT NULL PRIMARY KEY,
    profile_hash VARCHAR(128) NOT NULL,
    effective_at TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL,
    dynamic_profit_lock_enabled BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    profit_lock_activation_percent DECIMAL(12,6) NOT NULL,
    profit_lock_initial_percent DECIMAL(12,6) NOT NULL,
    profit_lock_trail_step_percent DECIMAL(12,6) NOT NULL,
    fixed_notional_usdt DECIMAL(30,12) NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
    INDEX idx_replay_policy_profile_hash (profile_hash)
);

-- FIX-156: immutable fixture manifest; payloads stay outside Production tables.
CREATE TABLE replay_fixture (
    id BIGINT NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    run_id BIGINT NOT NULL,
    fixture_type VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
    fixture_hash VARCHAR(128) NOT NULL,
    payload_json JSON NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
    UNIQUE KEY uq_replay_fixture_run_type_hash (run_id, fixture_type, fixture_hash),
    INDEX idx_replay_fixture_run_type (run_id, fixture_type)
);

-- FIX-156: Replay-only parity/outcome publication; never a Production trading record.
CREATE TABLE replay_run_output (
    id BIGINT NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    run_id BIGINT NOT NULL,
    result_type VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
    result_status VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
    details_json JSON NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
    INDEX idx_replay_run_output_run_type (run_id, result_type)
);

CREATE TABLE replay_decision_trace (
    id BIGINT NOT NULL AUTO_INCREMENT PRIMARY KEY,
    run_id BIGINT NOT NULL,
    decision_key VARCHAR(160) NOT NULL,
    decision_at TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL,
    decision_path VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
    expected_json JSON NULL,
    actual_json JSON NULL,
    result_status VARCHAR(64) NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMP(6) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(6),
    UNIQUE KEY uq_replay_decision_trace (run_id, decision_key),
    INDEX idx_replay_decision_trace_run_at (run_id, decision_at)
);
package com.crypto.regression.replay;

import java.util.Locale;
import java.util.Set;

/**
 * FIX-156: central allow-list for Replay-owned SQL names. This is deliberately
 * independent from Production repositories and must be used by Replay-local stores.
 */
public final class ReplayAccessPolicy {
    private static final Set<String> REPLAY_TABLES = Set.of(
            "replay_policy_profile",
            "replay_fixture",
            "replay_run_output",
            "replay_decision_trace");

    private ReplayAccessPolicy() {
    }

    public static void requireReplayTable(String table) {
        String normalized = table == null ? "" : table.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);
        if (!REPLAY_TABLES.contains(normalized)) {
            throw new ReplayForbiddenDependencyException("table=" + table);
        }
    }
}
package com.crypto.regression.replay;

import org.springframework.jdbc.core.JdbcTemplate;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Repository;

import java.sql.Timestamp;

/** FIX-156: Replay-only persistence for an immutable historical policy profile. */
@Repository
public class ReplayPolicyProfileStore {
    private final JdbcTemplate jdbc;

    public ReplayPolicyProfileStore(JdbcTemplate jdbc) {
        this.jdbc = jdbc;
    }

    public ReplayPolicyProfile require(long runId) {
        // FIX-156: never fall back to wallet_settings or current administration values.
        ReplayAccessPolicy.requireReplayTable("replay_policy_profile");
        return jdbc.query("""
                SELECT run_id, profile_hash, effective_at, dynamic_profit_lock_enabled,
                       profit_lock_activation_percent, profit_lock_initial_percent,
                       profit_lock_trail_step_percent, fixed_notional_usdt
                FROM replay_policy_profile WHERE run_id=?
                """, rs -> rs.next() ? new ReplayPolicyProfile(
                rs.getLong("run_id"), rs.getString("profile_hash"),
                rs.getTimestamp("effective_at").toInstant(),
                rs.getBoolean("dynamic_profit_lock_enabled"),
                rs.getBigDecimal("profit_lock_activation_percent"),
                rs.getBigDecimal("profit_lock_initial_percent"),
                rs.getBigDecimal("profit_lock_trail_step_percent"),
                rs.getBigDecimal("fixed_notional_usdt")) : null, runId);
    }

    public void insert(ReplayPolicyProfile profile) {
        // FIX-156: fixtures are prepared outside the Replay execution boundary.
        ReplayAccessPolicy.requireReplayTable("replay_policy_profile");
        int inserted = jdbc.update("""
                INSERT INTO replay_policy_profile
                (run_id, profile_hash, effective_at, dynamic_profit_lock_enabled,
                 profit_lock_activation_percent, profit_lock_initial_percent,
                 profit_lock_trail_step_percent, fixed_notional_usdt)
                VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)
                """, profile.runId(), profile.profileHash(), Timestamp.from(profile.effectiveAt()),
                profile.dynamicProfitLockEnabled(), profile.profitLockActivationPercent(),
                profile.profitLockInitialPercent(), profile.profitLockTrailStepPercent(),
                profile.fixedNotionalUsdt());
        if (inserted != 1) {
            throw new IllegalStateException("FIX-156 could not persist Replay policy profile");
        }
    }
}

5: import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;

7: import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;

13: import java.time.Instant;
14: import java.util.concurrent.Executor;
15: import java.util.concurrent.RejectedExecutionException;
